Periods of Drinfeld modules and local shtukas with complex multiplication
Urs Hartl, Rajneesh Kumar Singh

Abstract
Colmez [ Col93 ] conjectured a product formula for periods of abelian varieties over number fields with complex multiplication and proved it in some cases. His conjecture is equivalent to a formula for the Faltings height of CM abelian varieties in terms of the logarithmic derivatives at s = 0 s=0 of certain Artin L L -functions. In a series of articles we investigate the analog of Colmez’s theory in the arithmetic of function fields. There abelian varieties are replaced by Drinfeld modules and their higher dimensional generalizations, so-called A A -motives. In the present article we prove the product formula for the Carlitz module and we compute the valuations of the periods of a CM A A -motive at all finite places in terms of Artin L L -series. The latter is achieved by investigating the local shtukas associated with the A A -motive. Mathematics Subject Classification (2000) : 11G09, (11R42, 11R58, 14L05)



1
Introduction

In
[
Col93
]
P. Colmez considers product formulas for periods of abelian varieties. Let
X
be an abelian variety defined over a number field
K
with complex multiplication by the ring of integers in a CM-field
E
and of CM-type
\Phi
. Let
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\rm alg}
be the algebraic closure of
{\mathbb{Q}}
in
{\mathbb{C}}
, let
H_{E}:=\Hom_{\mathbb{Q}}(E,{\mathbb{Q}}^{\rm alg})
be the set of all ring homomorphisms
E\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^{\rm alg}
and assume that
K
contains
\psi(E)
for every
\psi\in H_{E}
. For a
\psi\in H_{E}
let
\omega_{\psi}\in\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}(X,K)
be a non-zero cohomology class such that
a^{*}\omega_{\psi}=\psi(a)\cdot\omega_{\psi}
for all
a\in E
. For every embedding
\eta\colon K\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^{\rm alg}
, let
X^{\eta}
and
\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}
be deduced from
X
and
\omega_{\psi}
by base extension. Let
(u_{\eta})_{\eta}\in\prod_{\eta\in H_{K}}\Koh_{1}(X^{\eta}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Z}})
be a family of cycles compatible with complex conjugation. Let
v
be a place of
{\mathbb{Q}}
. If
v=\infty
the de Rham isomorphism between Betti and de Rham cohomology yields a complex number
\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}
and its absolute value
\bigl|\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\bigr|_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. If
v
corresponds to a prime number
p\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we fix an inclusion
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\rm alg}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\rm alg}
. With this data Colmez
[
Col93
]
associates a period
\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}
in Fontaine’s
p
-adic period field
{\mathbf{B}}_{{\rm dR}}
and an absolute value
\bigl|\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\bigr|_{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. He considers the product
\prod_{v}\prod_{\eta\in H_{K}}\bigl|\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\bigr|_{v}
and (after some modifications) conjectures that this product evaluates to
1
; see
[
Col93
, Conjecture 0.1]
for the precise formulation. This conjecture is equivalent to a conjectural formula for the Faltings height of a CM abelian variety in terms of the logarithmic derivatives at
s=0
of certain Artin
L
-functions. Colmez proves the conjectures when
E
is an abelian extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}
. On the way, he computes
\prod_{\eta\in H_{K}}\bigl|\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\bigr|_{v}
at a finite place
v
in terms of the local factor at
v
of the Artin
L
-series associated with an Artin character
a^{0}_{E,\psi,\Phi}\colon\Gal({\mathbb{Q}}^{\rm alg}/{\mathbb{Q}})\to{\mathbb{C}}
that only depends on
E
,
\psi
and
\Phi
but not on
X
and
v
; see
[
Col93
, Théorème I.3.15]
. There has been further progress on Colmez’s conjecture by Obus
[
Obu13
]
, Yang
[
Yan13
]
, Andreatta, Goren, Howard, Madapusi Pera
[
AGHM15
]
, Yuan, Zhang
[
YZ15
]
, Barquero-Sanchez, Masri
[
BSM16
]
and others.

Our goal in this article is to develop the analog of Colmez’s theory in the “Arithmetic of function fields”. Here abelian varieties are replaced by Drinfeld modules
[
Dri76
,
Gos96
]
and their higher dimensional generalizations, so-called
A
-motives, which also generalize Anderson’s
t
-motives

[
And86
]
. To define them let
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
be a finite field with
q
elements, let
C
be a smooth projective, geometrically irreducible curve over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, let
\infty\in C
be a fixed closed point and let
A:=\Gamma(C\smallsetminus\{\infty\},{\cal{O}}_{C})
be the ring of regular functions on
C
outside
\infty
. Let
Q
be the fraction field of
A
and let
K
be a finite field extension of
Q
contained in a fixed algebraic closure
Q^{\rm alg}
of
Q
. We write
A_{K}:=A\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}K
and consider the endomorphism
\sigma:={\rm id}_{A}\otimes\Frob_{q,K}
of
A_{K}
, where
\Frob_{q,K}(b)=b^{q}
for
b\in K
. For an
A_{K}
-module
M
we set
\sigma^{*}M:=M\otimes_{A_{K},\sigma}A_{K}
and for a homomorphism
f\colon M\to N
of
A_{K}
-modules we set
\sigma^{*}f:=f\otimes{\rm id}_{A_{K}}\colon\sigma^{*}M\to\sigma^{*}N
. Let
\gamma\colon A\to K
be the inclusion
A\subset Q\subset K
, and set
{\cal{J}}:=(a\otimes 1-1\otimes\gamma(a)\colon a\in A)\subset A_{K}
. Then
\gamma
can be recovered as the homomorphism
A\to A_{K}/{\cal{J}}=K
.

Definition 1.1
.

An
A
-motive of rank
r
over
K
is a pair
{\underline{M\!}\,}=(M,\tau_{M})
consisting of a locally free
A_{K}
-module
M
of rank
r
and an isomorphism
\tau_{M}\colon\sigma^{*}M|_{\Spec A_{K}\smallsetminus\Var({\cal{J}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}M|_{\Spec A_{K}\smallsetminus\Var({\cal{J}})}
of the associated sheaves outside
\Var({\cal{J}})\subset\Spec A_{K}
. We write
\rk{\underline{M\!}\,}:=r
. A
morphism
between
A
-motives
f\colon(M,\tau_{M})\to(N,\tau_{N})
is an
A_{K}
-homomorphism
f\colon M\to N
with
f\circ\tau_{M}=\tau_{N}\circ\sigma^{*}f
.

Let us first give a rough sketch of the function field analog of Colmez’s conjecture, before we explain more details and our main results later in this introduction. An
A
-motive has various (co-)homology realizations, for example a
de Rham realization

\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
, and if it is uniformizable also a
Betti realization

\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,},A)
. For every
place
v
of
Q
, that is a closed point
v\in C
there is a comparison isomorphism between the Betti and de Rham cohomology of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
, which for
\omega\in\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
and
u\in\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,},A)
is given by a pairing
\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v}
and allows to define the absolute value
\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{v}:=\bigl|\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v}\bigr|_{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Now we say that
{\underline{M\!}\,}
has
complex multiplication
if
\QEnd_{K}({\underline{M\!}\,}):=\End_{K}({\underline{M\!}\,})\otimes_{A}Q
contains a commutative, semi-simple
Q
-algebra
E
of dimension
\dim_{Q}E=\rk{\underline{M\!}\,}
. Here semi-simple means that
E
is a product of fields and we do not assume that
E
is itself a field. Let
{\underline{M\!}\,}
be a uniformizable
A
-motive over a finite Galois extension
K\subset Q^{\rm alg}
of
Q
, which has complex multiplication by a
separable

Q
-algebra
E
. Let
0\neq\omega_{\psi}\in\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
satisfy
a^{*}\omega_{\psi}=\psi(a)\cdot\omega_{\psi}
for all
a\in E
, where
\psi:E\to K
is a
Q
-homomorphism. Then in Theorem
5.24
and Corollary
5.25
we will for all finite places
v
compute
\bigl|\int_{u}\omega_{\psi}\bigr|_{v}
and its average over all
Q
-automorphisms of
K
in terms of the local factor at
v
of an Artin
L
-series. The question analogous to
[
Col93
]
is then, whether one can make sense of the product
\prod_{v}\bigl|\int_{u}\omega_{\psi}\bigr|_{v}
over all places
v
including
\infty
, and whether this product evaluates to
1
.

After this vague sketch let us give more details and precise definitions in order to formulate our main results. We start by introducing the cohomology realizations of an
A
-motive
{\underline{M\!}\,}
over
K
. First of all, there is the
de Rham realization

\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K):=\sigma^{*}M/{\cal{J}}\cdot\sigma^{*}M
and for each maximal ideal
v\subset A
a
v
-adic étale realization

\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{M\!}\,},A_{v})
where
A_{v}
is the
v
-adic completion of
A
; see Definition
3.3
below. We let
Q_{v}
be the fraction field of
A_{v}
, and we let
Q_{\infty}
be the
\infty
-adic completion of
Q
and
{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}
be the completion of a fixed algebraic closure of
Q_{\infty}
. We fix a
Q
-embedding
Q^{\rm alg}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}
and consider the base extension of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
to
{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}
. There is a notion of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
being
uniformizable
and a uniformizable
{\underline{M\!}\,}
has a
Betti realization

\Koh^{1}_{\rm Betti}({\underline{M\!}\,},A)
; see
[
HJ16
, § 3.5]
. These realizations are related by period isomorphisms

\displaystyle h_{{\rm Betti},\,v}\colon\Koh^{1}_{\rm Betti}({\underline{M\!}\,},A)\otimes_{A}A_{v}\;

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\;\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{M\!}\,},A_{v})\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle h_{{\rm Betti},\,{\rm dR}}\colon\Koh^{1}_{\rm Betti}({\underline{M\!}\,},A)\otimes_{A}{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\;\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)\otimes_{K}{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}\;;

see
[
HJ16
, Theorem 3.23]
. Also for every place
v
of
Q
, let
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}
be its residue field and set
q_{v}:=\#{\mathbb{F}}_{v}=q^{[{\mathbb{F}}_{v}:{\mathbb{F}}_{q}]}
. Let
z:=z_{v}\in Q
be a uniformizing parameter at
v
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism
A_{v}={\mathbb{F}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
. Let
\zeta:=\zeta_{v}:=\gamma(z_{v})
denote the image of
z_{v}
in
K
. We simply write
z
, resp.
\zeta
for the elements
z\otimes 1
, resp.
1\otimes\zeta
of
Q\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}K
. Then the power series ring
K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
in the “variable”
z-\zeta
is canonically isomorphic to the completion of the local ring of
C_{K}:=C\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}K
at
\Var({\cal{J}})
; see
[
HJ16
, Lemma 1.2 and 1.3]
, and thus independent of
v
. We always consider the embedding
Q\hookrightarrow K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
given by
z\mapsto z=\zeta+(z-\zeta)
. The de Rham realization lifts to
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}):=\sigma^{*}M\otimes_{A_{K}}K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
, which is the analog of the (conjectural)
q
-de Rham cohomology of Bhatt, Morrow and Scholze
[
BMS15
,
BMS16
,
Sch16
]
, and the vector space
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})[\tfrac{1}{z-\zeta}]
over the field
K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}:=K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}[\tfrac{1}{z-\zeta}]
contains the
K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-lattice
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\underline{M\!}\,}:=\tau_{M}^{-1}(M\otimes_{A_{K}}K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
, which is called the
Hodge-Pink lattice of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
and is the analog of the Hodge-filtration of an abelian variety; see
[
HK15
, Remark 5.13]
.

If
v\neq\infty
we also fix a
Q
-embedding of
Q^{\rm alg}
into a fixed algebraic closure
Q_{v}^{\rm alg}
of
Q_{v}
and we let
{\mathbb{C}}_{v}
be the
v
-adic completion of
Q_{v}^{\rm alg}
. Again we denote the image of
z_{v}
in
Q_{v}^{\rm alg}
and
{\mathbb{C}}_{v}
by
\zeta_{v}
. We let
K_{v}\subset Q_{v}^{\rm alg}
be the induced completion of
K
and we let
R
be its valuation ring. There is a period isomorphism by
[
HK15
, Remark 4.16]

h_{v,{\rm dR}}\colon\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{M\!}\,},A_{v})\otimes_{A_{v}}{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\;\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}\bigr)\otimes_{K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\,.

The field
{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}
is the analog of Fontaine’s
p
-adic period field
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}
; see
[
HK15
, Remark 4.17]
.

Let
{\underline{M\!}\,}
have complex multiplication by a commutative, semi-simple
Q
-algebra
E
of dimension
\dim_{Q}E=\rk{\underline{M\!}\,}
. Let
{\cal{O}}_{E}
be the integral closure of
A
in
E
. It is a locally free
A
-module of
\rk_{A}{\cal{O}}_{E}=\dim_{Q}E
. We let
H_{E}:=\Hom_{Q}(E,Q^{\rm alg})
be the set of
Q
-homomorphisms
\psi\colon E\to Q^{\rm alg}
and we assume that
K
contains
\psi(E)
for every
\psi\in H_{E}
. Then by Lemma
A.3
in the appendix there is a decomposition
E\otimes_{Q}K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}=\prod_{\psi\in H_{E}}K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
, where
y_{\psi}
is a uniformizer at a place of
E
such that
\psi(y_{\psi})\neq 0
. Again by
[
HJ16
, Lemma 1.2 and 1.3]
the factors are obtained as the completion of
{\cal{O}}_{E}\otimes_{A}A_{K}={\cal{O}}_{E}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}K
along the kernels
(a\otimes 1-1\otimes\psi(a)\colon a\in{\cal{O}}_{E})
of the homomorphisms
\psi\otimes{\rm id}_{K}\colon{\cal{O}}_{E}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}K\to K
for
\psi\in H_{E}
.
Correspondingly
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
decomposes into eigenspaces

\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\;:=\;\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\otimes_{E\otimes_{Q}K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}\,K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}

each of which is free of rank
1
over
K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
. There are integers
d_{\psi}
such that the Hodge-Pink lattice is
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\underline{M\!}\,}=\prod_{\psi}(y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}))^{-d_{\psi}}\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
. The tuple
\Phi:=(d_{\psi})_{\psi\in H_{E}}
is the
CM-type
of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
.

If we fix elements
u\in\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,},Q):=\Hom_{A}\bigl(\Koh^{1}_{\rm Betti}({\underline{M\!}\,},A),Q\bigr)
and
\omega\in\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
we can define

\displaystyle\langle\omega,u\rangle_{\infty}\;:=\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle u\otimes{\rm id}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}}\bigl(h_{{\rm Betti},{\rm dR}}^{-1}(\omega\;{\rm mod}\;z-\zeta)\bigr)\;\in\;{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}\qquad\text{and}

(1.1)

\displaystyle\bigl|\textstyle\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{\infty}\;:=\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle\bigl|\langle\omega,u\rangle_{\infty}\bigr|_{\infty}\;\in\;{\mathbb{R}}\,,

(1.2)

where
|\,.\,|_{v}
is the normalized absolute value on
{\mathbb{C}}_{v}
with
|\zeta_{v}|_{v}=(\#{\mathbb{F}}_{v})^{-1}=q_{v}^{-1}
for every place
v
. We also consider the valuation
v\colon{\mathbb{C}}_{v}^{\scriptscriptstyle\times}\to{\mathbb{Q}}
on
{\mathbb{C}}_{v}
with
v(\zeta_{v})=1
. The expressions in (
1.1
) and (
1.2
) only depend on the image of
\omega
in
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
. Also at a finite place
v\neq\infty
of
Q
we consider on elements
x\neq 0
of the discretely valued field
{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}
the valuation
\hat{v}(x):=\ord_{z_{v}-\zeta_{v}}(x)
, and in addition we define

\displaystyle|x|_{v}:=\bigl|\bigl((z_{v}-\zeta_{v})^{-\hat{v}(x)}\cdot x\bigr)\;{\rm mod}\;z_{v}-\zeta_{v}\bigr|_{v}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle v(x):=-\log|x|_{v}\,/\log q_{v}\qquad\text{induced from}

\displaystyle\bigl((z_{v}-\zeta_{v})^{-\hat{v}(x)}\cdot x\bigr)\;{\rm mod}\;z_{v}-\zeta_{v}\in{\mathbb{C}}_{v}\,.

Note that
|x|_{v}
and
v(x)
are not a norm, respectively a valuation, because they do not satisfy the triangle inequality. The value
|x|_{v}
does not depend on the choice of the uniformizer
z_{v}
of
A_{v}
, because if
\tilde{z}_{v}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}z_{v}^{n}=:f(z_{v})
with
b_{n}\in{\mathbb{F}}_{v}
is another uniformizer and
\tilde{\zeta}_{v}=f(\zeta_{v})
, then
\tfrac{\tilde{z}_{v}-\tilde{\zeta}_{v}}{z_{v}-\zeta_{v}}\equiv f^{\prime}(\zeta_{v})\;{\rm mod}\;z_{v}-\zeta_{v}
in
{\cal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{v}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}={\mathbb{F}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}{\widehat{\otimes}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v},\gamma}{\cal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{v}}
by Lemma
A.1
in the appendix and
f^{\prime}(\zeta_{v})\in{\mathbb{F}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\times}
with inverse
\tfrac{dz_{v}}{d\tilde{z}_{v}}\big|_{\tilde{z}_{v}=\tilde{\zeta}_{v}}
.We define

\displaystyle\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v}\;:=\;

\displaystyle u\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}}\bigl(h_{{\rm Betti},v}^{-1}\circ h_{v,{\rm dR}}^{-1}(\omega)\bigr)\;\in\;{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\qquad\text{and}

(1.3)

\displaystyle\textstyle\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{v}\;:=\;

\displaystyle\bigl|\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v}\bigr|_{v}\;:=\;\bigl|\bigl((z_{v}-\zeta_{v})^{-\hat{v}(\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v})}\cdot\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v}\bigr)\;{\rm mod}\;z_{v}-\zeta_{v}\bigr|_{v}\;\in\;{\mathbb{R}}\,.

(1.4)

We will show in Theorem
5.24
below that if
E
is separable over
Q
and if
\omega\in\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
has non-zero image in
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
, then the absolute value (
1.4
) only depends on that image.

With these definitions we can now consider the product
\prod_{v}\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{v}
, or equivalently its logarithm
\log\prod_{v}\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{v}=-\sum_{v}v\bigl(\int_{u}\omega\bigr)\log q_{v}
. Like in Colmez’s theory, these products or sums do not converge and one has to give a convergent interpretation to their finite parts
\prod_{v\neq\infty}\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{v}
, respectively
-\sum_{v\neq\infty}v\bigl(\int_{u}\omega\bigr)\log q_{v}
; see Convention
1.4
below. To formulate the convention we make the following

Definition 1.2
.

For
F=Q
or
F=Q_{v}
let
F^{\rm sep}
be the separable closure of
F
in
F^{\rm alg}
and let
{\mathscr{G}}_{F}:=\Gal(F^{\rm sep}/F)
. For a finite field extension
F^{\prime}
of
F
let
H_{F^{\prime}}:=\Hom_{F}(F^{\prime},F^{\rm alg})
be the set of
F
-homomorphisms
\psi\colon F^{\prime}\to F^{\rm alg}
. Let
{\cal{C}}({\mathscr{G}}_{F},{\mathbb{Q}})
be the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-vector space of locally constant functions
a\colon{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\to{\mathbb{Q}}
and let
{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{F},{\mathbb{Q}})
be the subspace of those functions which are constant on conjugacy classes, that is, which satisfy
a(h^{-1}gh)=a(g)
for all
g,h\in{\mathscr{G}}_{F}
. Then the
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector space
{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{F},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{C}}
is spanned by the traces of representations
\rho\colon{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\to\GL_{n}({\mathbb{C}})
with open kernel for varying
n
by
[
Ser77
, § 2.5, Theorem 6]
. Via the fixed embedding
Q^{\rm sep}\hookrightarrow Q_{v}^{\rm sep}
we consider the induced inclusion
{\mathscr{G}}_{Q_{v}}\subset{\mathscr{G}}_{Q}
and morphism
{\cal{C}}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})\to{\cal{C}}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q_{v}},{\mathbb{Q}})
. If
\chi
is the trace of a representation
\rho\colon{\mathscr{G}}_{Q}\to\GL_{n}({\mathbb{C}})
with open kernel we let
L(\chi,s):=\prod_{\text{all }v}L_{v}(\chi,s)
, respectively
L^{\infty}(\chi,s):=\prod_{v\neq\infty}L_{v}(\chi,s)
be the Artin
L
-function of
\rho
, respectively without the factor at
\infty
. It only depends on
\chi
and converges for all
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
{\cal{R}}e(s)>1
; see
[
Ros02
, pp. 126ff]
. We also set

\displaystyle Z(\chi,s)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\tfrac{d}{ds}L(\chi,s)}{L(\chi,s)}\;=\;-\sum_{\text{all }v}Z_{v}(\chi,s)\log q_{v}\qquad\text{and}

(1.5)

\displaystyle Z^{\infty}(\chi,s)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\tfrac{d}{ds}L^{\infty}(\chi,s)}{L^{\infty}(\chi,s)}\;=\;-\sum_{v\neq\infty}Z_{v}(\chi,s)\log q_{v}\qquad\text{with}

(1.6)

\displaystyle Z_{v}(\chi,s)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\tfrac{d}{ds}L_{v}(\chi,s)}{-L_{v}(\chi,s)\cdot\log q_{v}}\;=\;\frac{\tfrac{d}{dq_{v}^{-s}}L_{v}(\chi,s)}{q_{v}^{s}\cdot L_{v}(\chi,s)}\;.

(1.7)

Moreover, we let
{\mathfrak{f}}_{\chi}
be the Artin conductor of
\chi
. It is an effective divisor
{\mathfrak{f}}_{\chi}=\sum_{v}\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(\chi)\cdot(v)
on
C
; see
[
Ser79
, Chapter VI, §§ 2,3]
, where
\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(\chi)
is denoted
f(\chi,v)
. We set

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm Art}(\chi)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\deg({\mathfrak{f}}_{\chi})\log q\;:=\;\sum_{\text{all }v}\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(\chi)[{\mathbb{F}}_{v}:{\mathbb{F}}_{q}]\log q\;=\;\sum_{\text{all }v}\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(\chi)\log q_{v}\quad\text{and}

(1.8)

\displaystyle\mu^{\infty}_{\rm Art}(\chi)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{v\neq\infty}\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(\chi)\log q_{v}\,.

(1.9)

In particular, only finitely many values
\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(\chi)
are non-zero. By linearity we extend
Z^{\infty}(\,.\,,s)
and
\mu^{\infty}_{\rm Art}
to all
a\in{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})
and
Z_{v}(\,.\,,s)
and
\mu_{{\rm Art},v}
to all
a\in{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q_{v}},{\mathbb{Q}})
. The map
Z_{v}(\,.\,,s)
takes values in
{\mathbb{Q}}(q_{v}^{-s})
.

In terms of this definition we prove in this article a formula for
\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{v}
with
v\neq\infty
for a uniformizable
A
-motive
{\underline{M\!}\,}
over
K
with complex multiplication by a semi-simple
separable
CM-algebra
E
of CM-type
\Phi=(d_{\varphi})_{\varphi\in H_{E}}
as follows. Let us assume that
{\cal{O}}_{E}\subset\End_{K}({\underline{M\!}\,})
, that
K
is a finite Galois extension of
Q
which contains
\psi(E)
for all
\psi\in H_{E}
, and that
{\underline{M\!}\,}
has good reduction at all primes of
K
. (By unpublished results of Schindler
[
Sch09
]
this is no restriction of generality, because for every
A
-motive
{\underline{M\!}\,}^{\prime}
with complex multiplication by a semi-simple separable CM-algebra
E
there is an
A
-motive
{\underline{M\!}\,}
isogenous to
{\underline{M\!}\,}^{\prime}
such that the integral closure
{\cal{O}}_{E}
of
A
in
E
is contained in
\End_{K}({\underline{M\!}\,})
and
{\underline{M\!}\,}^{\prime}
and
{\underline{M\!}\,}
have good reduction everywhere after replacing
K
by a finite separable extension. Moreover, every
A
-motive over a field extension of
Q
with
{\cal{O}}_{E}\subset\End_{K}({\underline{M\!}\,})
is already defined over a finite separable extension
K
of
Q
). For
\psi\in H_{E}
we define the functions

\displaystyle a_{E,\psi,\Phi}\colon\;{\mathscr{G}}_{Q}\to{\mathbb{Z}},

\displaystyle\quad g\mapsto d_{g\psi}\qquad\text{and}

(1.10)

\displaystyle a^{0}_{E,\psi,\Phi}\colon\;{\mathscr{G}}_{Q}\to{\mathbb{Q}},

\displaystyle\quad g\mapsto\tfrac{1}{\#H_{K}}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\eta\in H_{K}}d_{\eta^{-1}g\eta\psi}

(1.11)

which factor through
\Gal(K/Q)
. In particular,
a_{E,\psi,\Phi}\in{\cal{C}}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})
and
a^{0}_{E,\psi,\Phi}\in{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})
is independent of
K
.

Note that
\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,},Q)
is a free
E
-module of rank
1
by
[
BH09
, Lemma 7.2]
and that the eigenspace
\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)\,:=\,\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})/\bigl(y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi})\bigr)\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
in
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
of the character
\psi\colon E\to K
is a
K
-vector space of dimension
1
by Proposition
4.9
below. For an
E
-generator
u\in\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,},Q)
and a generator
\omega_{\psi}\in\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
as
K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-module we next define integers
v(\omega_{\psi})
and
v_{\psi}(u)
for all
v\neq\infty
which are all zero except for finitely many. Let
{\cal{O}}_{E_{v}}:={\cal{O}}_{E}\otimes_{A}A_{v}
and let
c\in E_{v}:=E\otimes_{Q}Q_{v}
be such that
c^{-1}u
is an
{\cal{O}}_{E_{v}}
-generator of
\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,},A)\otimes_{A}A_{v}
, which exists because
{\cal{O}}_{E_{v}}
is a product of discrete valuation rings. Then
c
is unique up to multiplication by an element of
{\cal{O}}_{E_{v}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\times}
and we set

\displaystyle v_{\psi}(u)\;:=\;v

\displaystyle\bigl(\psi(c)\bigr)\;\in\;{\mathbb{Z}}\,,

(1.12)

(NOTE THAT
v_{\psi}(u)\;\in\;{\mathbb{Q}}
IN GENERAL; SEE ERRATUM
B.1
)

where we extend
\psi\in H_{E}
by continuity to
\psi\colon E_{v}\to Q_{v}^{\rm alg}
. Also let
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}=({\cal{M}},\tau_{\cal{M}})
be an
A
-motive over
R:={\cal{O}}_{K_{v}}
with good reduction and
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{{\cal{O}}_{K_{v}}}K_{v}\cong{\underline{M\!}\,}\otimes_{K}K_{v}
; see Example
3.2
. Then there is an element
x\in K_{v}^{\scriptscriptstyle\times}
, unique up to multiplication by
R^{\scriptscriptstyle\times}
, such that
x^{-1}\omega_{\psi}\;{\rm mod}\;y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi})
is an
R
-generator of the free
R
-module of rank one

\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}},R)\;:=\;\bigl\{\omega\in\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}},R):=\sigma^{*}{\cal{M}}\otimes_{A_{R},\,\gamma\otimes{\rm id}_{R}}R\colon[b]^{*}\omega=\psi(b)\cdot\omega\kern 5.0pt\forall\;b\in{\cal{O}}_{E}\bigr\}\,,

and we set

\displaystyle v(\omega_{\psi})\;:=\;

\displaystyle v(x)\;\in\;{\mathbb{Z}}\,.

(1.13)

(NOTE THAT THIS DEFINITION IS WRONG; SEE ERRATUM
B.2
)

This value only depends on the image of
\omega_{\psi}
in
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},K)
. It also does not depend on the choice of the model
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
with good reduction, because all such models are isomorphic over
R
by
[
Gar03
, Proposition 2.13(ii)]
. In this situation our first main result is the following

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
\omega_{\psi}
be a generator of the
K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-module
\Koh^{\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\psi}-\psi(y_{\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
. For every
\eta\in H_{K}
let
{\underline{M\!}\,}^{\eta}
and
\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\in\Koh^{\eta\psi}({\underline{M\!}\,}^{\eta},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}y_{\eta\psi}-\eta\psi(y_{\eta\psi}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
be obtained by extension of scalars via
\eta
, and choose an
E
-generator
u_{\eta}\in\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{M\!}\,}^{\eta},Q)
. Then for every place
v\neq\infty
of
C
we have

\textstyle\tfrac{1}{\#H_{K}}\sum\limits_{\eta\in H_{K}}v({\textstyle\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}})\;=\;Z_{v}(a^{0}_{E,\psi,\Phi},1)-\mu_{{\rm Art},v}(a^{0}_{E,\psi,\Phi})-\dfrac{v({\mathfrak{d}}_{\psi(E)/Q})}{[\psi(E):Q]}+\tfrac{1}{\#H_{K}}\sum\limits_{\eta\in H_{K}}\bigl(v(\omega_{\psi}^{\eta})+v_{\eta\psi}(u_{\eta})\bigr)\,,

where
{\mathfrak{d}}_{\psi(E)/Q}
is the discriminant of the field extension
\psi(E)/Q
.

We will prove this theorem at the end of Section
5
by using the
local shtuka
at
v
attached to
{\underline{M\!}\,}
. The latter is an analog of the Dieudonné-module of the
p
-divisible group attached to an abelian variety; see
[
Har09
, § 3.2]
. The theorem allows us to make the following convention which is the analog of
[
Col93
, Convention 0]
.

Convention 1.4
.

Let
(x_{v})_{v\neq\infty}
be a tuple of complex numbers indexed by the finite places
v
of
Q
. We will give a sense to the (divergent) series
\Sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\sum_{v\neq\infty}x_{v}
in the following situation. We suppose that there exists an element
a\in{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
x_{v}=-Z_{v}(a,1)\log q_{v}
for all
v
except for finitely many. Then we let
a^{*}\in{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})
be defined by
a^{*}(g):=a(g^{-1})
. We further assume that
Z^{\infty}(a^{*},s)
does not have a pole at
s=0
, and we define the limit of the series
\sum_{v\neq\infty}x_{v}
as

\Sigma\;:=\;-Z^{\infty}(a^{*},0)-\mu^{\infty}_{\rm Art}(a)+\sum_{v\neq\infty}\bigl(x_{v}+Z_{v}(a,1)\log q_{v}\bigr)

(1.14)

inspired by Weil’s
[
Wei48
, p. 82]
functional equation

Z(\chi,1-s)\;=\;-Z(\chi^{*},s)-(2\cdot genus(C)-2)\chi(1)\log q-\mu_{\rm Art}(\chi)

deprived of the summands at
\infty
, where the genus term is considered as belonging to
\infty
.

The Convention
1.4
and the Theorem
1.3
allow us to give a convergent interpretation to the sum
-\sum_{v}\sum_{\eta\in H_{K}}v\bigl(\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\bigr)\log q_{v}
and the product
\prod_{v}\prod_{\eta\in H_{K}}\bigl|\int_{u_{\eta}}\omega_{\psi}^{\eta}\bigr|_{v}
, and we can ask whether this product is
1
. In Section
2
we prove our second main result, namely that the answer to the question is “yes” in the easiest case of the
Carlitz motive
which is related to the zeta function of
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[t]
and is the analog of the multiplicative group
{\mathbb{G}}_{m,{\mathbb{Q}}}
considered by Colmez. For general CM
A
-motives we plan to address the question in a sequel to this article and also discuss its consequences for the Faltings height of CM
A
-motives similar to
[
Col93
, Théorème 0.3 and Conjecture 0.4]
and conditions under which
Z^{\infty}(a^{*},s)
does not have a pole at
s=0
.

Let us describe the structure of this article. In Section
3
we recall from
[
HK15
]
the definition of local shtukas, how to attach a local shtuka at
v\subset A
to an
A
-motive
{\underline{M\!}\,}
over
L
with good reduction, and we discuss its cohomology realizations. In Section
4
we define the notions of complex multiplication and CM-type of a local shtuka, and in Section
5
we compute the periods and their valuations of a local shtuka with complex multiplication, and we prove Theorem
1.3
. Finally in Appendix
A
we prove the facts used above.

2
The Carlitz-Motive

Let
A={\mathbb{F}}_{q}[t]
and
C={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
. Let
K={\mathbb{F}}_{q}(\vartheta)
be the rational function field in the variable
\vartheta
and let
\gamma:A\to K
be given by
\gamma(t)=\vartheta
. We also set
z:=z_{\infty}:=\tfrac{1}{t}
and
\zeta:=\zeta_{\infty}:=\tfrac{1}{\vartheta}
. It satisfies
|\zeta|_{\infty}=q^{-1}<1
. The
Carlitz motive
over
K
is the
A
-motive
{\underline{{\cal{C}}\!}\,}=(K[t],\tau_{\cal{C}}=t-\vartheta)
which is associated with the Carlitz module; see
[
Car35
]
or
[
Gos96
, Chapter 3]
. It has rank
1
and dimension
1
, and complex multiplication by the ring of integers
A
in
E:=Q
with CM-type
\Phi=(d_{\rm id})
, where
H_{E}=\{{\rm id}\}
and
d_{\rm id}=1
. As is well known, its cohomology satisfies
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{{\cal{C}}\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})=K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
and
\Koh^{1}_{\rm Betti}({\underline{{\cal{C}}\!}\,},A)=A\cdot\beta\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta}
, where
\beta\in{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}
satisfies
\beta^{q-1}=-\zeta
and
\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta}:=\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}(1-\zeta^{q^{i}}t)
; see for example
[
HJ16
, Example 3.34]
. We denote the generator
1
of
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{{\cal{C}}\!}\,},K{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})
by
\omega
and we take
u\in\Koh_{1,{\rm Betti}}({\underline{{\cal{C}}\!}\,},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[t])
as the generator which is dual to
\beta\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta}\in\Koh^{1}_{\rm Betti}({\underline{{\cal{C}}\!}\,},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[t])
. The de Rham isomorphism
h_{{\rm Betti},{\rm dR}}
sends
\beta\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta}
to

\sigma^{*}(\beta\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta})\cdot\omega\;=\;\beta^{q}\sigma^{*}(\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta})\cdot\omega\;\in\;\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\;=\;{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}\cdot\omega,

respectively to
\beta^{q}\sigma^{*}(\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle-}_{\zeta})|_{t=\vartheta}\cdot\omega=\beta^{q}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}(1-\zeta^{q^{i}-1})\cdot\omega\in\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},{\mathbb{C}}_{\infty})={\mathbb{C}}_{\infty}\cdot\omega
. Here the coefficient
\beta^{q}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}(1-\zeta^{q^{i}-1})
is the function field analog of the complex number
(2i\pi)^{-1}
, the inverse of the period of the multiplicative group
{\mathbb{G}}_{m,{\mathbb{Q}}}
. We obtain

\textstyle\bigl|\int_{u}\omega\bigr|_{\infty}\;=\;\bigl|\bigl(\beta^{q}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}(1-\zeta^{q^{i}-1})\bigr)^{-1}\bigr|_{\infty}\;=\;|\beta|_{\infty}^{-q}\;=\;q^{q/(q-1)}.

At a finite place
v\subset{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[t]
let
v=(z_{v})
and
\zeta_{v}=\gamma(z_{v})
. Then
\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{M\!}\,},A_{v})=A_{v}\cdot(\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle+}_{\zeta_{v}})^{-1}
, where
\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle+}_{\zeta_{v}}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\ell_{n}z_{v}^{n}\in{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
with
\ell_{0}^{q_{v}-1}=-\zeta_{v}
and
\ell_{n}^{q_{v}}+\zeta_{v}\ell_{n}=\ell_{n-1}
; see
[
HK15
, Example 4.19]
. This implies
|\ell_{n}|=|\zeta_{v}|^{q_{v}^{-n}/(q_{v}-1)}<1
. The
v
-adic comparison isomorphism
h_{v,{\rm dR}}
sends the generator
\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle+}_{\zeta_{v}}
of
\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{M\!}\,},A_{v})
to

(z_{v}-\zeta_{v})^{-1}(\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle+}_{\zeta_{v}})^{-1}\cdot\omega\;\in\;\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\underline{M\!}\,},{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\bigr)\;=\;{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z_{v}-\zeta_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\cdot\omega,

where the coefficient of
\omega
is the
v
-adic Carlitz period
which has a pole of order one at
z_{v}=\zeta_{v}
. So
\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v}=(z_{v}-\zeta_{v})\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle+}_{\zeta_{v}}
has
\hat{v}(\langle\omega,u\rangle_{v})=1
and

\textstyle\bigl|\int_{u}\omega|_{v}\;=\;\bigl|\ell^{\scriptscriptstyle+}_{\zeta_{v}}\;{\rm mod}\;z_{v}-\zeta_{v}\bigr|_{v}\;=\;\bigl|\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\ell_{n}\zeta_{v}^{n}\bigr|_{v}\;=\;|\ell_{0}|_{v}\;=\;q_{v}^{-1/(q_{v}-1)}

So the product
\prod_{v}\bigl|{\textstyle\int_{u}\omega}\bigr|_{v}
of the norms at all places has logarithm

\log\prod_{\text{all }v}\bigl|{\textstyle\int_{u}\omega}\bigr|_{v}\;=\;\log\bigl|{\textstyle\int_{u}\omega}\bigr|_{\infty}+\log\prod_{v\neq\infty}\bigl|{\textstyle\int_{u}\omega}\bigr|_{v}\;=\;\frac{q}{q-1}\log q+\sum_{v\neq\infty}\frac{-1}{q_{v}-1}\log q_{v}\,.

(2.1)

Note that this series is not convergent, but that the sum over
v\neq\infty
is equal to
\frac{\zeta^{\prime}_{A}(1)}{\zeta_{A}(1)}
and the summand at
\infty
is equal to
\frac{\zeta^{\prime}_{A}(0)}{\zeta_{A}(0)}
, where
\zeta_{A}
is the zeta function associated with
A
, which does not converge at
s=1
. Namely, the zeta functions are defined as the following products which converge for
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
{\cal{R}}e(s)>1

\displaystyle\zeta_{C}(s)\;:=\;\prod_{\text{all }v}(1-(\#{\mathbb{F}}_{v})^{-s})^{-1}\;=\;\prod_{\text{all }v}(1-q_{v}^{-s})^{-1}\;=\;\frac{1}{(1-q^{-s})(1-q^{1-s})}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\zeta_{A}(s)\;:=\;\prod_{v\neq\infty}(1-(\#{\mathbb{F}}_{v})^{-s})^{-1}\;=\;\prod_{v\neq\infty}(1-q_{v}^{-s})^{-1}\;=\;\frac{1}{1-q^{1-s}}\ ;

see for example
[
Sil86
, Chapter V, Example 2.1]
. In particular
(1-q_{v}^{-s})^{-1}=L_{v}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}},1)
and
\zeta_{A}(s)=L^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}},s)
where
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}}\colon g\mapsto 1
is the trivial character in
{\cal{C}}^{0}({\mathscr{G}}_{Q},{\mathbb{Q}})
, which equals
a_{Q,{\rm id},\Phi}=a^{0}_{Q,{\rm id},\Phi}
. Then
\frac{1}{q_{v}^{s}-1}=Z_{v}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}},s)
and
\tfrac{\zeta_{A}^{\prime}(s)}{\zeta_{A}(s)}=Z^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}},s)
in the notation of Definition
1.2
. Applying Convention
1.4
with
a={\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}}
and
\mu_{\rm Art}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}})=0
and
genus({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}})=0
we define the limit of the series as

\displaystyle\frac{q}{q-1}\log q+\sum_{v\neq\infty}\frac{-1}{q_{v}-1}\log q_{v}

\displaystyle:=\frac{q}{q-1}\log q-\sum_{v\neq\infty}Z_{v}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}},1)\log q_{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{q}{q-1}\log q-Z^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\rm 1\kern-2.7ptl\kern 0.9pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.3ptl\kern 0.4pt}}{\hbox{\scriptsize\rm 1\kern-2.4ptl\kern 0.5pt}}},0)

\displaystyle=\frac{q}{q-1}\log q-\frac{\zeta^{\prime}_{A}(0)}{\zeta_{A}(0)}

\displaystyle=0.

So the value of the product
\prod_{v}\bigl|{\textstyle\int_{u}\omega}\bigr|_{v}
is
1
for the Carlitz motive.

3
Local Shtukas

In the rest of the article we fix a place
v\neq\infty
of
Q
. We keep the notation from the introduction, except that we write
L=\kappa{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}\pi_{L}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}
for the field
K_{v}
and let
R=\kappa{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}\pi_{L}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
be its valuation ring. We write
z:=z_{v}
. Then
A_{v}={\mathbb{F}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
and
Q_{v}={\mathbb{F}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}
. The homomorphism
\gamma\colon A\to K
extends by continuity to
\gamma\colon A_{v}\to L
and factors through
\gamma\colon A_{v}\to R
with
\zeta:=\zeta_{v}=\gamma(z)\in\pi_{L}R\smallsetminus\{0\}
. Let
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
be the power series ring in the variable
z
over
R
and
\hat{\sigma}
the endomorphism of
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
with
\hat{\sigma}(z)=z
and
\hat{\sigma}(b)=b^{q_{v}}
for
b\in R
, where
q_{v}=\#{\mathbb{F}}_{v}
. For an
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-module
\hat{M}
we let
\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}:=\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}},\hat{\sigma}^{*}}R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
as well as
\hat{M}[\frac{1}{z-\zeta}]:=\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}[\frac{1}{z-\zeta}]
and
\hat{M}[\frac{1}{z}]:=\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}[\frac{1}{z}]
.

Definition 3.1
.

A
local
\hat{\sigma}
-shtuka of rank
r
over
R
is a pair
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}=(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}})
consisting of a free
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-module
\hat{M}
of rank
r
, and an isomorphism
\tau_{\hat{M}}:\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}[\frac{1}{z-\zeta}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\hat{M}[\frac{1}{z-\zeta}]
. It is
effective
if
\tau_{\hat{M}}(\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M})\subset\hat{M}
and
étale
if
\tau_{\hat{M}}(\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M})=\hat{M}
. We write
\rk{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}
for the rank of
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}
.

A
morphism
of local shtukas
f:{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}=(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}})\to{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,}=(\hat{N},\tau_{\hat{N}})
over
R
is a morphism of the underlying modules
f:\hat{M}\to\hat{N}
which satisfies
\tau_{\hat{N}}\circ\hat{\sigma}^{*}f=f\circ\tau_{\hat{M}}
. We denote the
A_{v}
-module of homomorphisms
f\colon{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}\to{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,}
by
\Hom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,})
and write
\End_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,})=\Hom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,})
.

A
quasi-morphism
between local shtukas
f\colon(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}})\to(\hat{N},\tau_{\hat{N}})
over
R
is a morphism of
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}[\tfrac{1}{z}]
-modules
f\colon M[\tfrac{1}{z}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}N[\tfrac{1}{z}]
with
\tau_{\hat{N}}\circ\hat{\sigma}^{*}f=f\circ\tau_{\hat{M}}
. It is called a
quasi-isogeny
if it is an isomorphism of
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}[\tfrac{1}{z}]
-modules. We denote the
Q_{v}
-vector space of quasi-morphisms from
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}
to
{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,}
by
\QHom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,})
and write
\QEnd_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,})=\QHom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,})
.

Note that
\Hom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,})
is a finite free
A_{v}
-module of rank at most
\rk{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}\cdot\rk{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,}
by
[
HK15
, Corollary 4.5]
and
\QHom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,})=\Hom_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,})\otimes_{A_{v}}Q_{v}
. Also every quasi-isogeny
f\colon{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}\to{\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,}
induces an isomorphism of
Q_{v}
-algebras
\QEnd_{R}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\QEnd_{R}({\underline{\hat{N}\!}\,})
,
g\mapsto fgf^{-1}
.

Example 3.2
.

We assume that the
A
-motive
{\underline{M\!}\,}=(M,\tau_{M})
has
good reduction
, that is, there exist a pair
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}=({\cal{M}},\tau_{{\cal{M}}})
consisting of a locally free module
{\cal{M}}
over
A_{R}:=A\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}R
of finite rank and an isomorphism
\tau_{\cal{M}}:\sigma^{*}{\cal{M}}|_{\Spec A_{R}\smallsetminus\Var({\cal{J}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}{\cal{M}}|_{\Spec A_{R}\smallsetminus\Var({\cal{J}})}
of the associated sheaves outside the vanishing locus
\Var({\cal{J}})\subset\Spec A_{R}
of the ideal
{\cal{J}}:=(a\otimes 1-1\otimes\gamma(a):\ a\in A)\subset A_{R}
, such that
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{R}L\cong{\underline{M\!}\,}
. The reduction
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{R}\kappa
is an
A
-motive over
\kappa
of
A
-characteristic
v=\ker(\gamma:A\to\kappa)
. The pair
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
is called an
A
-motive over
R
and a
good model
of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
.

We consider the
v
-adic completions
A_{v,R}
of
A_{R}
and
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}:=({\cal{M}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R},\tau_{\cal{M}}\otimes{\rm id})
of
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
. We let
d_{v}:=[{\mathbb{F}}_{v}:{\mathbb{F}}_{q}]
and discuss the two cases
d_{v}=1
and
d_{v}>1
separately. If
d_{v}=1
, and hence
q_{v}=q
and
\hat{\sigma}=\sigma
, we have
A_{v,R}=R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
, and
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}
is a local
\hat{\sigma}
-shtuka over
\Spec R
which we denote by
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}_{v}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}})
and call the
local shtuka at
v
associated with
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
.

If
d_{v}>1
, the situation is more complicated, because
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}R
and
A_{v,R}
fail to be integral domains. Namely,

{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}R=\prod_{\Gal({\mathbb{F}}_{v}/{\mathbb{F}}_{q})}{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}R=\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/d_{v}{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}R\,/\,(a\otimes 1-1\otimes\gamma(a)^{q^{i}}:a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{v})

and
\sigma
transports the
i
-th factor to the
(i+1)
-th factor. In particular
\hat{\sigma}
stabilizes each factor. Denote by
{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}
the ideal of
A_{v,R}
generated by
\{a\otimes 1-1\otimes\gamma(a)^{q^{i}}:a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\}
. Then

A_{v,R}=\prod_{\Gal({\mathbb{F}}_{v}/{\mathbb{F}}_{q})}A_{v}{\widehat{\otimes}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}R=\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/d_{v}{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{v,R}\,/\,{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}.

Note that each factor is isomorphic to
R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
and the ideals
{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}
correspond precisely to the places
v_{i}
of
C_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}
lying above
v
. The ideal
{\cal{J}}
decomposes as follows
{\cal{J}}\cdot A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=(z-\zeta)
and
{\cal{J}}\cdot A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}=(1)
for
i\neq 0
. We define the
local shtuka at
v
associated with
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
as
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}_{v}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}}):=(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}}):=\bigl({\cal{M}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\,,\,(\tau_{\cal{M}}\otimes 1)^{d_{v}}\bigr)
, where
\tau_{\cal{M}}^{d_{v}}:=\tau_{\cal{M}}\circ\sigma^{*}\tau_{\cal{M}}\circ\ldots\circ\sigma^{(d_{v}-1)*}\tau_{\cal{M}}
. Of course if
d_{v}=1
we get back the definition of
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}_{v}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}})
given above. Also note if
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
is effective, then
{\cal{M}}/\tau_{\cal{M}}(\sigma^{*}{\cal{M}})=\hat{M}/\tau_{\hat{M}}(\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M})
.

The local shtuka
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}_{v}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}})
allows to recover
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}
via the isomorphism

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d_{v}-1}(\tau_{\cal{M}}\otimes 1)^{i}\;{\rm mod}\;{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}\colon\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d_{v}-1}\sigma^{i*}({\cal{M}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}),\;(\tau_{\cal{M}}\otimes 1)^{d_{v}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\neq 0}{\rm id}\Bigr)\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\;{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}\,,

because for
i\neq 0
the equality
{\cal{J}}\!\cdot\!A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}=(1)
implies that
\tau_{\cal{M}}\otimes 1
is an isomorphism modulo
{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}
; see
[
BH11
, Propositions 8.8 and 8.5]
for more details.

Next we define the
v
-adic realization and the de Rham realization of a local shtuka
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}=(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}})
over
R
. Since
\tau_{\hat{M}}
induces an isomorphism
\tau_{\hat{M}}:\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
, because
z-\zeta\in L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\times}
, we can think of
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
as an étale local shtuka over
L
.

Definition 3.3
.

The
v
-adic realization

\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},A_{v})
of a local
\hat{\sigma}
-shtuka
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}=(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}})
is the
{\mathscr{G}}_{L}:=\Gal(L^{{\rm sep}}/L)
-module of
\tau
- invariants

\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},A_{v})\;:=\;(\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L^{\rm sep}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})^{\tau}:=\{m\in\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L^{\rm sep}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}:\tau_{\hat{M}}(\hat{\sigma}_{\!\hat{M}}^{*}m)=m\},

where we set
\hat{\sigma}_{\!\hat{M}}^{*}m:=m\otimes 1\in\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}},\hat{\sigma}}R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}=:\sigma^{*}M
for
m\in M
. One also writes sometimes
\check{T}_{v}{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}=\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},A_{v})
and calls this the
dual Tate module of
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}
. By
[
HK15
, Proposition 4.2]
it is a free
A_{v}
-module of the same rank as
\hat{M}
. We also write
\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},B):=\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},A_{v})\otimes_{A_{v}}B
for an
A_{v}
-algebra
B
.

If
{\underline{M\!}\,}=(M,\tau_{M})
is an
A
-motive over
L
with good model
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
and
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}={\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}_{v}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}})
is the local shtuka at
v
associated with
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
, then
\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},A_{v})
is by
[
HK15
, Proposition 4.6]
canonically isomorphic as a representation of
{\mathscr{G}}_{L}
to the
v
-adic realization of
{\underline{M\!}\,}
, which is defined as

\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{M\!}\,},A_{v})\;:=\;\{m\in M\otimes_{A_{L}}A_{v,L^{\rm sep}}\colon\tau_{M}(\sigma_{\!M}^{*}m)=m\},

where we set
\sigma_{\!M}^{*}m:=m\otimes 1\in M\otimes_{A_{R},\sigma}A_{R}=:\sigma^{*}M
for
m\in M
and where
A_{v,L^{\rm sep}}
is the
v
-adic completion of
A_{L^{\rm sep}}
.

Definition 3.4
.

Let
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}=(\hat{M},\tau_{\hat{M}})
be a local
\hat{\sigma}
-shtuka over
R
. We define the
de Rham realizations
of
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}
as

\displaystyle\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},R)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}/(z-\zeta)\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}\;=\;\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}},z\mapsto\zeta}R\,,\quad\text{as well as}

\displaystyle\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\;:=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L)\;:=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{*}\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}},z\mapsto\zeta}L\;=\;\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\otimes_{L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}/(z-\zeta)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},R)\otimes_{R}L\,.

It carries the
Hodge-Pink lattice

{\mathfrak{q}}^{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}:=\tau_{\hat{M}}^{-1}(\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\subset\Koh^{1}_{{\rm dR}}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})[\tfrac{1}{z-\zeta}]
. We also write
\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},B):=\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}})\otimes_{L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}B
for an
L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-algebra
B
.

If
{\underline{M\!}\,}=(M,\tau_{M})
is an
A
-motive over
L
with good model
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
and
{\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}={\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,}_{v}({\underline{{\cal{M}}}})
is the local shtuka at
v
associated with
{\underline{{\cal{M}}}}
and
d_{v}=[{\mathbb{F}}_{v}:{\mathbb{F}}_{q}]
is as in Example
3.2
, the map

\sigma^{*}\tau_{M}^{{d_{v}}-1}=\sigma^{*}\tau_{M}\circ\sigma^{2*}\tau_{M}\circ\cdots\circ\sigma^{({d_{v}}-1)*}\tau_{M}\colon\sigma^{{d_{v}}*}M\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\sigma^{*}M\otimes_{A_{R}}A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}

is an isomorphism, because
\tau_{M}
is an isomorphism over
A_{v,R}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}
for all
i\neq 0
. Therefore it defines canonical isomorphisms of the de Rham realizations

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\tau_{M}^{{d_{v}}-1}\colon

\displaystyle\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\underline{M\!}\,},L{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}\bigr)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\tau_{M}^{{d_{v}}-1}\colon

\displaystyle\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}({\underline{M\!}\,},L)\,,

which are compatible with the Hodge-Pink lattices.

Remark 3.5
.

By
[
HK15
, Theorem 4.15]
there is a canonical comparison isomorphism

h_{v,{\rm dR}}\colon\;\Koh^{1}_{v}\bigl({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\bigr)\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mbox{\hskip 2.84526pt\raisebox{3.98337pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\hskip-9.95845pt$\longrightarrow$}}}\;\Koh^{1}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},{\mathbb{C}}_{v}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\rm(\hskip-1.49994pt(}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm(\hskip-1.19579pt(}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm(\hskip-1.0208pt(}}}z-\zeta{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\rm)\hskip-1.49994pt)}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm)\hskip-1.19579pt)}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm)\hskip-1.0208pt)}}}\bigr)

(3.1)

which is equivariant for the action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{L}
. For our computations below we need an explicit description of
h_{v,{\rm dR}}
. It is constructed as follows. The natural inclusion
\Koh^{1}_{v}({\underline{\hat{M}\!}\,},A_{v})\subset\hat{M}\otimes_{R{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}}L^{\rm sep}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
defines a canonical isomorphism of
L^{\rm sep}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\rm[\hskip-1.49994pt[}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm[\hskip-1.19579pt[}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm[\hskip-1.0208pt[}}}z{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\rm]\hskip-1.49994pt]}}{\mbox{\scriptsize\rm]\hskip-1.19579pt]}}{\mbox{\tiny\rm]\hskip-1.0208pt]}}}
-modules
